With which leave-taking I waited coldly till they gave me my due salutation, and then walked out of the banqueting-hall without offering a soul another glance. I took my way to the grand gate of the pyramid, called for the officer of the guard, and demanded exit. The man was obsequious enough, but he opened with some demur.

===Chinese===

那人的道谢，从残破的嘴里含混地滚出来，旁边有几个人装出一副嫌恶，抖缩不迭。我黑着眉头转向他们：「列位大人，你们的慈悲，看来同你们的胆量一样微薄。泽蒙的话，你们装出一副漂亮的不信；对他的祭司身份，你们抿着嘴轻蔑；可轮到要对他下手时，你们又显出一副谨慎来——这种谨慎，放在旧日，我们本有个难听的叫法。我宁可做塔尔卡，带着他一身的不洁，也不愿做你们中间任何一个，就照你们此刻站着的这副模样。」
作过这番辞别，我冷冷候着，直到他们向我行了应得的礼数，才走出宴会厅，再不朝任何一个人看一眼。我径直走到金字塔大门，唤来卫队军官，要求放我出去。那人倒是恭顺，开口却带着几分迟疑。

===Original===

“My lord’s attendants have not yet come up?”
“I have none.”
“My lord knows the state of the streets?”
“I did twenty years back. I shall be able to pick my way.”
“My lord must remember that the city is beleaguered,” the fellow persisted. “The people are hungry. They prowl in bands after nightfall, and﻿—I make no question that my lord would conquer in a fight against whatever odds, but﻿—”
“Quite right. I covet no street scuffle tonight. Lend me, I pray you, a sufficiency of men. You will know best what are needed. For me, I am accustomed to a city with quiet streets.”

===Chinese===

「大人的随从还没有上来？」
「我没有随从。」
「大人可知道眼下街上的光景？」
「二十年前还知道。我自己拣得着路。」
「大人须记得，这城正遭围困，」那人仍不死心。「百姓都在挨饿。入夜之后，他们成群结伙地在街上游荡，而且——我毫不怀疑，任凭怎样的人多势众，大人也必能取胜，可是——」
「正是如此。今夜我不贪一场街头殴斗。请你借我一队够用的人手。需要多少，你最清楚。至于我，我习惯的城池，街道是安静的。」

===Original===

A score of sturdy fellows were detailed off for my escort, and with them in a double file on either hand, I marched out from the close perfumed air of the pyramid into the cool moonlight of the city. It was my purpose to make a tour of the walls and to find out somewhat of the disposition of these rebels.
But the Gods saw fit to give me another education first. The city, as I saw it during that night walk, was no longer the old capital that I had known, the just accretion of the ages, the due admixture of comfort and splendour. The splendour was there, vastly increased. Whole wards had been swept away to make space for new palaces, and new pyramids of the wealthy, and I could not but have an admiration for the skill and the brain which made possible such splendid monuments.

===Chinese===

二十名壮健的汉子被拨来做我的护卫，他们在我左右排成两列，我便从金字塔里密闭的香风中开拔出去，走进城中清凉的月光。我的用意，是沿着城墙巡行一周，探一探这些叛军的布置。
可诸神认为，还得先给我受一番别的教育。那一夜步行所见的城，已不再是我旧识的那座老都城——岁月层层叠叠正当的积淀，安适与华彩两下合宜的掺和。华彩还在，且大大加增了。整片整片的坊区被扫荡一空，腾出地方来营造新宫殿、营造富户们的新金字塔，我不由得不佩服那份技艺与头脑，竟能使这般壮丽的丰碑立得起来。

===Original===

And, indeed, gazing at them there under the silver of the moonlight, I could almost understand the emotions of the Europeans and other barbarous savages which cause them to worship all such great buildings as Gods, since they deem them too wonderful and majestic to be set up by human hands unaided.
Still, if it was easy to admire, it was simple also to see plain advertisement of the cost at which these great works had been reared. From each grant of ground, where one of these stately piles earned silver under the moon, a hundred families had been evicted and left to harbour as they pleased in the open; and, as a consequence, now every niche had its quota of sleepers, and every shadow its squad of fierce wild creatures, ready to rush out and rob or slay all wayfarers of less force than their own.
Myself, I am no pamperer of the common people. I say that, if a man be left to hunger and shiver, he will work to gain him food and raiment; and if not, why then he can die, and the State is well rid of a worthless fellow. But here beside us, as we marched through many wards, were marks of blind oppression; starved dead bodies, with the bones starting through the lean skin, sprawled in the gutter; and indeed it was plain that, save for the favoured few, the people of the great capital were under a most heavy oppression.

===Chinese===

的确，在月光的银辉底下凝望着它们，我几乎能体谅欧洲人和别处蛮荒野人的那种心情了——他们把这一切巨构崇楼都当作神明来礼拜，只因他们认定，这些东西奇妙庄严得太甚，绝非人手独力所能建起。
然而，钦羡归钦羡，这些大工程是何等代价垒起来的，也同样一望便知，明明白白昭示在那里。月色之下，每一座赚得银辉的巍峨巨厦，其地皮之上都曾有一百户人家被扫地出门，任凭他们随意露宿旷野；于是如今，每一处壁龛都有它定额的睡客，每一片暗影都有它一伙凶悍的野人，只待扑出来，劫杀一切力不及己的过路客。
说到我自己，我绝非娇惯平民的人。依我说，一个人若由他挨饿受冻，他自会去做工挣他的衣食；若是不做，那他尽可以死去，国家正好甩脱一个废物。可是这一路穿行许多坊区，我们身旁尽是盲目暴虐的痕迹：饿毙的尸首，瘦皮底下绽出骨头，横陈在阴沟里；的确明明白白，这座大都城的百姓，除了得宠的少数，正受着极其沉重的压榨。

===Original===

But at this, though I might regret it abominably, I could make no strong complaint. By the ancient law of the land all the people, great and small, were the servants of the king, to be put without question to what purposes he chose; and Phorenice stood in the place of the king. So I tried to think no treason, but with a sigh passed on, keeping my eyes above the miseries and the squalors of the roadway, and sending out my thoughts to the stars which hung in the purple night above, and to the High Gods which dwelt amongst them, seeking, if it might be, for guidance for my future policies. And so in time the windings of the streets brought us to the walls, and, coursing beside these and giving fitting answer to the sentries who beat their drums as we passed, we came in time to that great gate which was a charge to the captain of the garrison.
Here it was plain there was some special commotion. A noise of laughter went up into the still night air, and with it now and again the snarl and roar of a great beast, and now and again the shriek of a hurt man. But whatever might be afoot, it was not a scene to come upon suddenly. The entrance gates of our great capital were designed by their ancient builders to be no less strong than the walls themselves. Four pairs of valves were there, each a monstrous block of stone two man-heights square, and a man-height thick, and the wall was doubled to receive them, enclosing an open circus between its two parts. The four gates themselves were set one at the inner, one at the outer side of each of these walls, and a hidden machinery so connected them, that of each set one could not open till the other was closed; and as for forcing them without war engines, one might as foolishly try to push down the royal pyramid with the bare hand.

===Chinese===

可对着这一切，我尽管可以痛惜到极点，却提不出什么有力的抗议。按这片国土的古法，万民无论贵贱，皆是君王的仆役，君王要派什么用场，不容置喙；而福伦尼刻如今正居君王之位。于是我竭力不去想叛逆的念头，叹息一声，继续前行，目光越过路面的凄惨腌臜，把心思送往高悬在紫夜上空的星辰，送往居于星辰之间的至上诸神，寻求——若可行的话——为我日后的方略指点一条明路。就这样，街巷几番回环，终于把我们引到了城墙；沿着墙根行进，对逢着我们经过便击鼓的哨兵，一一以应有的答话应对，我们终于来到归守城队长掌管的那座大门。
这里分明有一场特别的骚动。一阵阵笑声腾入静夜的空气，间或夹着巨兽的咆哮与低吼，又间或是一声受伤男子的惨叫。可不管里头正在干什么，那都不是可以贸然撞见的场面。我们大都城的入城诸门，古代的建造者设计得同城墙本身一般坚固。门共四对门扇，每一扇都是一方骇人的整块巨石，见方两人之高，厚一人之高；墙体也为此筑作两重，当中围出一片露天的圆场。四道门就分设在这两重墙各自的里侧与外侧，又有一套暗藏的机括把它们连锁起来：每组两门，一扇不闭死，另一扇便开不得；至于不用攻城重械便想强行破开它们，其愚蠢不亚于赤手空拳去推倒王家金字塔。

===Original===

My escort made outcry with the horn which hung from the wall inviting such a summons, and a warder came to an arrow-slit, and did inspection of our persons and business. His survey was according to the ancient form of words, which is long, and this was made still more tedious by the noise from within, which ever and again drowned all speech between us entirely.
But at last the formalities had been duly complied with, and he shot back the massive bars and bolts of stone, and threw ajar one monstrous stone valve of the door. Into the chamber within﻿—a chamber made from the thickness of the wall between the two doors﻿—I and my fellows crowded, and then the warder with his machines pulled to the valve which had been opened, and came to me again through the press of my escort, bowing low to the ground.

===Chinese===

我的护卫吹响墙上挂着的号角——那本是备人传唤之用的——一个守门人便来到一孔箭窗前，查验我们的来人与来意。他查问依照的是一套古老的例话，本就冗长，又被里头的喧闹搅得愈发难熬：那声浪时时把我们之间的问答整个淹没。
但礼数总算一一应酬完毕，他把粗大的石闩石销一一退开，把一扇骇人的石门扉推开一道缝。我和随从们便挤进门内的堂室——那屋子是拿两道门之间墙的厚度掏出来的——随后守门人用他的机括把开过的那扇门扉重新拉拢，又从护卫的人堆里挤到我面前，深深躬到地上。

===Original===

“I have no vail to give you,” I said abruptly. “Get on with your duty. Open me that other door.”
“With respect, my lord, it would be better that I should first announce my lord’s presence. There is a baiting going forward in the circus, and the tigers are as yet mere savages, and no respecters of persons.”
“The what?”
“The tigers, if my lord will permit them the name. They are baiting a batch of prisoners with the two great beasts which the Empress (whose name be adored) has sent here to aid us keep the gate. But if my lord will, there are the ward rooms leading off this passage, and the galleries which run out from them commanding the circus, and from there my lord can see the sport undisturbed.”

===Chinese===

「我没有赏钱给你，」我劈头便道，「办你的差事罢。给我开那边那道门。」
「恕小人直言，大人，还是容小人先替大人通禀一声为好。圆场里眼下正有一场纵兽咬囚，那几头虎还是十足的生番，全然不认尊卑。」
「什么虎？」
「是虎，大人若肯容它们冒用这个名字。眼下正用那两头巨兽，纵着咬一批囚徒——那两头兽，是女皇（愿她的圣名受颂赞）遣来助我们守门的。大人若愿意，这条甬道旁边设有卫房，卫房又伸出几条廊道，居高俯临圆场，大人在那里可以把这场把戏看个清净。」

===Original===

Now, the mere lust for killing excites only disgust in me, but I suspected the orders of the Empress in this matter, and had a curiosity to see her scheme. So I stepped into the warder’s lodge, and on into the galleries which commanded the circus with their arrow-slits. The old builders of the place had intended these for a second line of defence, for, supposing the outer doors all forced, an enemy could be speedily shot down in the circus, without being able to give a blow in return, and so would only march into a deathtrap. But as a gazing-place on a spectacle they were no less useful.
The circus was bright lit by the moonlight, and the air which came in to me from it was acrid with the reek of blood. There was no sport in what was going forward: as I said, it was mere killing, and the sight disgusted me. I am no prude about this matter. Give a prisoner his weapons, put him in a pit with beasts of reasonable strength, and let him fight to a finish if you choose, and I can look on there and applaud the strokes. The war prisoner, being a prisoner, has earned death by natural law, and prefers to get his last stroke in hot blood than to be knocked down by the headsman’s axe. And it is any brave man’s luxury either to help or watch a lusty fight. But this baiting in the circus between the gates was no fair battle like that.

===Chinese===

说到单纯的杀戮欲，那只能引起我的厌恶；可我疑心这是女皇在这件事上的旨意，倒存了一份好奇，想看看她的方略。于是我跨进守门人的值房，再走进那几条以箭窗俯临圆场的廊道。此地的古代建造者，原是把它们设计作第二道防线的：即便外门尽数被攻破，敌人也会在圆场里被迅速射倒，还不得还手一击，等于径自走进一座死陷阱。不过拿它们当作看把戏的观台，也同样的合用。
圆场被月光照得雪亮，从那边透到我面前的空气里，血腥呛人。眼下行的事里没有半点竞技可言：如我所说，那只是单纯的宰杀，这光景叫我作呕。在这件事上，我并非道学先生。给囚徒兵器，把他放进坑里，配上力量相当的兽，随你们高兴，让他斗出个结局，我可以立在一旁，为那些招式喝彩。战俘既已被俘，按自然之法已赚得一死，他也情愿趁着热血拼出最后一击，胜过被行刑人的斧头放倒。而下场相助也好，立观一场酣战也好，都是任何勇敢男子的一桩享受。可这两重门之间圆场上的这场纵兽，全不是那样的公平战斗。

===Original===

To begin with, the beasts were no fair antagonists for single men. In fact, twenty men armed might well have fled from them. When the warder said tigers, I supposed he meant the great cats of the woods. But here, in the circus, I saw a pair of the most terrific of all the fur-bearing land beasts, the great tigers of the caves﻿—huge monsters, of such ponderous strength that in hunger they will oftentimes drag down a mammoth, if they can find him away from his herd.
How they had been brought captive I could not tell. Hunter of beasts though I had been for all my days, I take no shame in saying that I always approached the slaying of a cave-tiger with stratagem and infinite caution. To entrap it alive and bring it to a city on a chain was beyond my most daring schemes, and I have been accredited with more new things than one. But here it was in fact, and I saw in these captive beasts a new certificate for Phorenice’s genius.
The purpose of these two cave-tigers was plain: whilst they were in the circus, and loose, no living being could cross from one gate to the other. They were a new and sturdy addition to the defences of the capital. A collar of bronze was round the throat of each, and on the collar was a massive chain which led to the wall, where it could be payed out or hauled in by means of a windlass in one of the hidden galleries. So that at ordinary moments the two huge beasts could be tethered, one close to either end of the circus, as the litter of bones and other messes showed, leaving free passageway between the two sets of doors.

===Chinese===

首先，那两头兽对孤身的人便不是公平的敌手。实说罢，便是二十个带兵器的汉子，也尽可以逃开它们。守门人说虎，我只当他说的是林间的大猫。可在这圆场上，我看见的却是一对毛皮陆兽中最骇人的东西——大洞虎：庞然巨怪，膂力沉猛到这般地步，饿极了的时候，只要寻得一头离群的猛犸，往往能把它扑翻拖倒。
它们是怎样被生擒来的，我无从猜测。尽管我终生以猎兽为业，我却毫不引以为耻地承认：每逢要去杀一头洞虎，我总是凭着计谋和无穷的小心行事。至于活捉生擒，再用链子把它牵进一座城里，那连我最胆大的方略也够不着——而世人算在我名下的新鲜事，原不止一件。可眼前这却是实情，在这两头被俘的巨兽身上，我看到了福伦尼刻天才的又一份新凭证。
这两头洞虎的用途一目了然：只要它们待在圆场里、撒开着，任何活物都休想从这道门走到那道门。它们是这座都城防务上新添的一路强援。每头颈上箍着一圈青铜项圈，项圈上连一条粗重的锁链，直通到墙里，由一条暗廊里的绞盘放出去、绞回来。如此，寻常时候，那两头巨兽便可分拴在圆场两头——满地狼藉的骨头和秽物便是明证——当中给两重门之间留出一条通畅的过道。

===Original===

But when I stood there by the arrow-slit, looking down into the moonlight of the circus, these chains were slackened (though men stood by the windlass of each), and the great striped brutes were prowling about the circus with the links clanking and chinking in their wake. Lying stark on the pavement were the bodies of some eight men, dead and uneaten; and though the cave-tigers stopped their prowlings now and again to nuzzle these, and beat them about with playful paw-blows, they made no pretence at commencing a meal. It was clear that this cruel sport had grown common to them, and they knew there were other victims yet to be added to the tally.
Presently, sure enough, as I watched, a valve of the farther gate swung back an arm’s length, and a prisoner, furiously resisting, was thrust out into the circus. He fell on his face, and after one look around him he lay resolutely still, with eyes on the ground passively awaiting his fate. The ponderous stone of the gate clapped to in its place; the cave-tigers turned in their prowlings; and a chatter of wagers ran to and fro amongst the watchers behind the arrow-slits.
It seemed there were niceties of cruelty in this wretched game. There was a sharp clank as the windlasses were manned, and the tethering chains were drawn in by perhaps a score of links. One of the cave-tigers crouched, lashed its tail, and launched forth on a terrific spring. The chain tautened, the massive links sang to the strain, and the great beast gave a roar which shook the walls. It had missed the prone man by a hand’s breadth, and the watchers behind the arrow-slits shrieked forth their delight. The other tiger sprang also and missed, and again there were shouts of pleasure, which mingled with the bellowing voices of the beasts. The man lay motionless in his form. One more cowardly, or one more brave, might have run from death, or faced it; but this poor prisoner chose the middle course﻿—he permitted death to come to him, and had enough of doggedness to wait for it without stir.

===Chinese===

可待我站到箭窗旁、俯瞰月色中的圆场时，那两条链子已经放松（两条绞盘边都守着人），两头像带斑纹的巨兽正在圆场里逡巡，链环在身后叮当乱响。铺石的地面上僵卧着约莫八具男人的尸首，死了，却未被吃过一口；洞虎虽然不时停下逡巡，去拱一拱他们，拿爪子玩笑似的拍打他们，却绝无开饭的意思。显然，这种残忍的把戏于它们早已成了家常，它们知道，这笔账上还有别的祭品要添进来。
果然，不多时，正当我望着，远门的一扇门扉荡开一臂之宽，一个死命挣扎的囚徒被推搡进圆场。他扑面跌倒，四下里望了一眼，便横下心一动不动地伏着，眼睛盯住地面，听天由命地等着他的结局。沉重的门石砰的一声阖回原位；洞虎在逡巡中转了向；箭窗后面的看客中间，一片下注的嚷嚷来回起落。
这场可怜的把戏里，竟还有残忍的精细讲究。绞盘边有人上手，铛啷一声脆响，拴兽的链子绞进来约莫二十个链环。一头洞虎蹲伏下去，尾巴猛抽，随即发动一记骇人的腾扑。链子绷直，粗重的链环吃劲发出尖鸣，那巨兽一声吼，震得墙体发颤。它离那个俯伏的人差了一掌宽，没有扑着，箭窗后的看客们尖声叫起好来。另一头虎也扑了一记，也没扑着，又是一片快活的呐喊，同兽吼搅作一处。那人伏在原地，一动不动。再怯懦些的人，或者再勇敢些的人，也许早从死跟前逃开，或者索性迎上去；可这可怜的囚徒拣了条中间的路——他由着死亡自己走过来，又有足够的倔劲，一动不动地等它。

===Original===

The great cave-tigers were used, it appeared, to this disgusting sport. There were no more wild springs, no more stubbings at the end of the massive chains. They lay down on the pavement, and presently began to purr, rolling on to their sides and rubbing themselves luxuriously. The prisoner still lay motionless in his form.
By slow degrees the monstrous brutes each drew to the end of its chain and began to reach at the man with outstretched forepaw. The male could not touch him; the female could just reach him with the far tip of a claw; and I saw a red scratch start up in the bare skin of his side at every stroke. But still the prisoner would not stir. It seemed to me that they must slack out more links of one of the tigers’ chains, or let the vile play linger into mere tediousness.
But I had more to learn yet. The male tiger, either taught by his own devilishness, or by those brutes that were his keepers, had still another ruse in store. He rose to his feet and turned round, backing against the chain. A yell of applause from the hidden men behind the arrow-slits told that they knew what was in store; and then the monstrous beast, stretched to the utmost of its vast length, kicked sharply with one hind paw.

===Chinese===

看样子，这两头大洞虎对这种恶心的把戏早已惯了。再没有狂野的腾扑，再没有粗链尽头那顿挫的冲刺。它们在铺石上卧了下来，不多时竟打起呼噜，翻过身侧躺着，舒舒服服地蹭起自己来。那囚徒仍旧伏在原地，一动不动。
那两头巨怪一点一点、各自挪到自家链子的尽头，伸出前爪去够那人。雄的够不着他；雌的爪尖恰好能够着他；每一爪下去，我便看见他腰间赤裸的皮肉上绽起一道红痕。可那囚徒仍旧不肯动弹。照我看，他们要么就得把哪一头虎的链子再放出几环，要么便任这出下流的戏，拖成纯粹的乏味。
可我还有得学呢。那头雄虎，或是由它自家的鬼邪调教出来，或是被那些看守它的畜生们教会，居然还藏着另一手花招。它站起身，转过去，倒顶着链子。箭窗后暗处的人一声喝彩，显见得他们知道接下来是什么；随即那头巨兽把庞大的身子抻到极处，一只后爪猛地一蹬。

===Original===

I heard the crunch of the prisoner’s ribs as the pads struck him, and at that same moment the poor wretch’s body was spurned away by the blow, as one might throw a fruit with the hand. But it did not travel far. It was clear that the she-tiger knew this manoeuvre of her mate’s. She caught the man on his bound, nuzzling over him for a minute, and then tossing him high into the air, and leaping up to the full of her splendid height after him.
Those other onlookers thought it magnificent; their gleeful shouts said as much. But for me, my gorge rose at the sight. Once the tigers had reached him, the man had been killed, it is true, without any unnecessary lingering. Even a light blow from those terrific paws would slay the strongest man living. But to see the two cave-tigers toying with the poor body was an insult to the pride of our race.
However, I was not there to preach the superiority of man to the beasts, and the indecency and degradation of permitting man to be unduly insulted. I had come to learn for myself the new balance of things in the kingdom of Atlantis, and so I stood at my place behind the arrow-slit with a still face. And presently another scene in this ghastly play was enacted.

===Chinese===

肉掌击中那人时，我听见他肋骨的碎响；同一瞬间，那可怜虫的身子被这一蹬抛了出去，像人随手抛一枚果子。可它没有飞远。显然，那头雌虎熟知它伴侣这一手。它趁那人腾在半空接住了他，在他身上嗅弄了一会儿，便把他高高抛上半空，又以它全部壮美的身量，腾身跃起，追着扑上去。
旁的看客只觉这一手漂亮极了；他们快活的喝彩把这话说得明明白白。可我看了，胃里直往上翻。诚然，虎一搭上他，那人便死了，没有多余的拖延——那样骇人的爪子，便是轻轻一击，也能结果世上最壮的汉子。可是眼看两头洞虎拿那具可怜的尸首耍弄，这是对我们人类尊严的一场侮辱。
不过，我到那里去，原不是为了宣讲人怎样高于兽、纵容人受过分的侮辱又是何等有伤体统、何等堕落。我是来亲自查明亚特兰蒂斯王国里新的轻重格局的，于是我照旧站在箭窗后的位子上，面无表情。不多时，这出怕人的戏又开演了新的一幕。

===Original===

The cave-tigers tired of their sport, and first one and then the other fell once more to prowling over the littered pavements, with the heavy chains scraping and chinking in their wake. They made no beginning to feast on the bodies provided for them. That would be for afterwards. In the present, the fascination of slaughter was big in them, and they had thought that it would be indulged further. It seemed that they knew their entertainers.
Again the windlass clanked, and the tethering chains drew the great beasts clear of the doorway; and again a valve of the farther door swung ajar, and another prisoner was thrust struggling into the circus. A sickness seized me when I saw that this was a woman, but still, in view of the object I had in hand, I made no interruption.
It was not that I had never seen women sent to death before. A general, who has done his fighting, must in his day have killed women equally with men; yes, and seen them earn their deathblow by lusty battling. Yet there seemed something so wanton in this cruel helpless sacrifice of a woman prisoner, that I had a struggle with myself to avoid interference. Still it is ever the case that the individual must be sacrificed to a policy, and so as I say, I watched on, outwardly cold and impassive.

===Chinese===

洞虎玩腻了它们的把戏，先是一头、随后另一头，又在一地狼藉的铺石上逡巡起来，沉重的链子在身后刮擦作响。它们没有动手去吃那些备给它们的尸首：那是留给之后的。眼下，屠杀的迷欲正在它们胸中高涨，它们也料想这迷欲还会再被纵容一阵。看来，它们认得那些款待它们的人。
绞盘又铛啷一响，拴链把两头巨兽从门口拖开；远门的一扇门扉又荡开一道缝，又一名囚徒被推搡着、挣扎着送进圆场。待我看清那是个女人，一阵恶心攫住了我；但念及我手里的目的，我仍没有出手干预。
倒不是我从未见过女人被送去处死。一个真正打过仗的将军，一生之中必然同杀男人一样杀过女人；是的，也见过她们凭酣烈的搏杀，赚得自己那一击之死。可是把一个女囚这样孤弱无助地送上残酷的祭坛，其中那份肆无忌惮，叫我同自己角力了一番，才按住了出手的冲动。个人总归要为方略牺牲，从来如此；于是如我所说，我继续旁观下去，外表冷漠，无动于衷。

===Original===

I watched too (I confess it freely) with a quickening heart. Here was no sullen submissive victim like the last. She may have been more cowardly (as some women are), she may have been braver (as many women have shown themselves); but, at any rate, it was clear that she was going to make a struggle for her life, and to do vicious damage, it might be, before she yielded it up. The watchers behind the arrow-slits recognized this. Their wagers, and the hum of their appreciation, swept loudly round the ring of the circus.
They stripped their prisoners, before they thrust them out to this death, of all the clothes they might carry, for clothes have a value; and so the woman stood there bare-limbed in the moonlight.
She clapped her back to the great stone door by which she had entered, and faced fate with glowing eye. Gods! there have been times in early years when I could have plucked out sword and jumped down, and fought for her there for the sheer delight of such a battle. But now policy restrained me. The individual might want a helping hand, but it was becoming more and more clear that Atlantis wanted a minister also; and before these great needs, the lesser ones perforce must perish. Still, be it noted that, if I did not jump down, no other man there that night had sufficient manhood remaining to venture the opportunity.

===Chinese===

而且我看着看着（我坦然承认），心跳也快了起来。这一个可不是方才那种闷声认命的祭品。她也许更怯懦些（有的女人如此），也许更大胆些（许多女人已这样显露过自己）；但无论如何，看得出她是要为这条性命拼一场了，而且在交出性命之前，说不定还要狠狠伤敌。箭窗后的看客们也看出了这一点。他们的赌注声，还有赞赏的低嗡，在圆场四下里响亮地起伏。
囚徒们被推出去送这场死之前，身上穿得动的衣服一律剥尽，因为衣裳是值钱的东西；于是那女人便赤着肢体，立在月光里。
她把脊背抵住进来的那扇巨石门，目光灼灼，直面自己的命运。诸神啊！早年的岁月里，我也曾有过那样的时刻：真能拔出剑、跳下去，就在那里为她而战，只为了那样一场战斗的纯粹快乐。可如今，是方略拘住了我。个人也许需要一只援手，可亚特兰蒂斯也同样需要一位大臣，这一点已越来越清楚；在这些大需要面前，小些的需要便只好消亡。不过有一点须得记下：我纵然没有跳下去，当夜在那里，也没有第二个男人剩下足够的丈夫气概，敢来抓这个机会。

===Original===

My heart glowed as I watched her. She picked a bone from the litter on the pavement and beat off its head by blows against the wall. Then with her teeth she fashioned the point to still further sharpness. I could see her teeth glisten white in the moonrays as she bit with them.
The huge cave-tigers, which stood as high as her head as they walked, came nearer to her in their prowlings, yet obviously neglected her. This was part of their accustomed scheme of torment, and the woman knew it well. There was something intolerable in their noiseless, ceaseless paddings over the pavement. I could see the prisoner’s breast heave as she watched them. A terror such as that would have made many a victim sick and helpless.
But this one was bolder than I had thought. She did not wait for a spring: she made the first attack herself. When the she-tiger made its stroll towards her, and was in the act of turning, she flung herself into a sudden leap, striking viciously at its eye with her sharpened bone. A roar from the onlookers acknowledged the stroke. The cave-tiger’s eye remained undarkened, but the puny weapon had dealt it a smart flesh wound, and with a great bellow of surprise and pain it scampered away to gain space for a rush and a spring.

===Chinese===

我望着她，心头滚热。她从铺石的狼藉里拾起一根骨头，对着墙连击几下，磕掉了骨头脑。随后又用牙齿，把骨尖整治得愈发锋利。她咬磨的时候，我看得见她的牙齿在月光里闪着白光。
那两头巨虎走动起来齐她的头高，逡巡着一步步挨近她，却明摆着并不理会她。这是它们惯常那一套折磨的一环，那女人也深知这一点。它们踩过铺石，无声无息、无休无止，那步态里有种叫人无法忍受的东西。我看得见囚徒的胸脯随着她的注视起伏。那样的恐怖，早叫许多祭品恶心瘫软了。
可这一个比我料想的更大胆。她不等虎扑，倒自家先动了手。雌虎踱到她跟前、正待转身的一刻，她猛地纵身一跃，抡起磨尖的骨头，狠狠直取它的眼睛。看客们一声吼，认下了这一击。洞虎的眼睛没有瞎，可那件微小的兵器，也给它划开一道火辣辣的皮肉伤；它又惊又痛，大吼一声，撒腿跑开，去腾出扑击的空当。

===Original===

But the woman did not await its charge. With a shrill scream she sped forward, running at the full of her speed across the moonlight directly towards that shadowed part of the encircling wall within whose thickness I had my gazing place; and then, throwing every tendon of her body into the spring, made the greatest leap that surely any human being ever accomplished, even when spurred on by the utmost of terror and desperation. In an after day I measured it, and though of a certainty she must have added much to the tally by the sheer force of her run, which drove her clinging up the rough surface of the wall, it is a sure thing that in that splendid leap her feet must have dangled a man-height and a half above the pavement.
I say it was prodigious, but then the spur was more than the ordinary, and the woman herself was far out of the common both in thews and intelligence; and the end of the leap left her with five fingers lodged in the sill of the arrow-slit from which I watched. Even then she must have slipped back if she had been left to herself, for the sill sloped, and the stone was finely smooth; but I shot out my hand and gripped hers by the wrist, and instantly she clambered up with both knees on the sills, and her fingers twined round to grip my wrist in her turn.
And now you will suppose she gushed out prayers and promises, thinking only of safety and enlargement. There was nothing of this. With savage panting wordlessness she took fresh grip on the sharpened bone with her spare hand, and lunged with it desperately through the arrow-slit. With the hand that clutched mine she drew me towards her, so as to give the blows the surer chance, and so unprepared was I for such an attack, and with such fierce suddenness did she deliver it, that the first blow was near giving me my quietus. But I grappled with the poor frantic creature as gently as might be﻿—the stone of the wall separating us always﻿—and stripped her of her weapon, and held her firmly captive till she might calm herself.

===Chinese===

可那女人不等它扑来。她一声尖啸，向前飞奔，以全副脚力横掠过月光，直朝环墙那片阴影扑去——我的观望处，便嵌在那段墙的厚度里；随即她把周身每一根筋腱都掷进这一跃，完成了必是人类古往今来所做过的最伟大的一跳，纵使被极端的恐怖与绝望驱迫时也一样。日后我曾实地量过：虽然她凭奔跑的冲势，贴着粗糙的墙面攀附而上，必定给这数目添了不少，但千真万确，就在那辉煌的一跃里，她的双脚必定曾荡在离铺石一人半之高的空中。
我说这一跳惊世骇俗，可那鞭策也非同寻常，而这女人本人，无论筋骨还是心智，也都远迈常人；这一跃的尽头，她的五根手指，正扣在我观望的那孔箭窗的窗台上。即便到那时，若全凭她自己，她也必定滑跌下去，因为窗台是斜的，石面又磨得极光；可我探出手去，一把攥住她的手腕；她登时双膝蹬上窗台攀了上来，手指反过来缠住我的手腕，也攥了个紧。
这时你必以为她会滔滔地祷告许愿，一心只求活命脱身。全然不是这样。她喘着野性的粗气，一言不发，腾出的那只手重新攥紧磨尖的骨头，隔着箭窗绝望地直刺过来。攥住我那只手把我往她跟前拖，好教这几下扎得更稳当。我对这样的袭击毫无防备，她出手又那么凶猛突兀，头一下险些就送了我的终。可我尽力温柔地同这可怜的疯乱生灵缠斗——中间始终隔着一堵墙的石料——缴了她的兵器，把她牢牢擒住，直到她平静下来。

===Original===

“That was an ungrateful blow,” I said. “But for my hand you’d have slipped and be the sport of a tiger’s paw this minute.”
“Oh, I must kill someone,” she panted, “before I am killed myself.”
“There will be time enough to think upon that some other day; but for now you are far enough off meeting further harm.”
“You are lying to me. You will throw me to the beasts as soon as I loose my grip. I know your kind: you will not be robbed of your sport.”
“I will go so far as to prove myself to you,” said I, and called out for the warder who had tended the doors below. “Bid those tigers be tethered on a shorter chain,” I ordered, “and then go yourself outside into the circus, and help this lady delicately to the ground.”

===Chinese===

「这一下可真没良心，」我说。「若不是我这只手，你早滑了下去，这会儿正给虎爪当玩意儿呢。」
「哼，我总得先杀了谁，」她喘着气说，「才轮到我自己被杀。」
「那件事，来日方长，够你想的；眼下嘛，你再遭什么祸害，还远着呢。」
「你在骗我。我一撒手，你就要把我丢给畜生。我认得你们这路人：你们这场把戏，是断不肯让人搅了的。」
「我不妨索性向你证明我自己，」我说，便扬声唤那位在下面管门的守门人。「把那两头虎拴上短些的链子，」我吩咐道，「然后你自己到外面圆场里去，小心把这位夫人搀扶下地。」

===Original===

The word was passed and these things were done; and I too came out into the circus and joined the woman, who stood waiting under the moonlight. But the others who had seen these doings were by no means suited at the change of plan. One of the great stone valves of the farther door opened hurriedly, and a man strode out, armed and flushed. “By all the Gods!” he shouted. “Who comes between me and my pastime?”
I stepped quietly to the advance. “I fear, sir,” I said, “that you must launch your anger against me. By accident I gave that woman sanctuary, and I had not heart to toss her back to your beasts.”
His fingers began to snap against his hilt.

===Chinese===

命令传下去，事情照办了；我也出到圆场里，走到那女人身旁，她正立在月光下等候。可那些眼见这一番作为的人，对这计划的变卦绝谈不上满意。远门的一扇巨石门扉慌忙荡开，一个佩着兵器、满面涨红的男人大步走了出来。「凭全体诸神起誓！」他吼道。「是谁插到我同我的消遣中间来？」
我安步上前。「恐怕，先生，」我说，「您这怒火只好朝我发了。我一时偶然，给了那女人一处庇身之所，实在不忍心把她扔回给您的畜生们。」
他的手指在剑柄上弹得啪啪作响。

===Original===

“You have come to the wrong market here with your qualms. I am captain here, and my word carries, subject only to Phorenice’s nod. Do you hear that? Do you know too that I can have you tossed to those striped gatekeepers of mine for meddling in here without an invitation?” He looked at me sharp enough, but saw plainly that I was a stranger. “But perhaps you carry a name, my man, which warrants your impertinence?”
“Deucalion is my poor name,” I said, “but I cannot expect you will know it. I am but newly landed here, sir, and when I left Atlantis some score of years back, a very different man to you held guard over these gates.” He had his forehead on my feet by this time. “I had it from the Empress this night that she will tomorrow make a new sorting of this kingdom’s dignities. Perhaps there is some recommendation you would wish me to lay before her in return for your courtesies?”

===Chinese===

「带着你那点良心上的顾忌，你可是进错了市面。我是这里的队长，我的话便是军令，上头只服福伦尼刻的一个点头。你听见没有？你可还知道，你不请自来、擅闯到这里，我有权把你丢给我那几头花斑的守门兽？」他把我端详得够锐利，却也看明白我是个生面孔。「不过，朋友，或许你身上带着一个名字，配得起你这份无礼？」
「德乌卡利翁，是我的贱名，」我说，「不过我不敢指望您听闻过它。先生，我才新近上岸；二十年前我离开亚特兰蒂斯的时候，看守这几道门的，还是位同您大不相同的人。」说话这工夫，他的额头已经贴上了我的脚背。「今夜我亲耳听女皇说，明日她要把这王国的品秩重新清点分派。您如此多礼，不知可有几句好话，想托我在她面前代陈？」

===Original===

“My lord,” said the man, “if you wish it, I can have a turn with those cave-tigers myself now, and you can look on from behind the walls and see them tear me.”
“Why tell me what is no news?”
“I wish to remind my lord of his power; I wish to beg of his clemency.”
“You showed your power to these poor prisoners; but from what remains here to be seen, few of them have tasted much of your clemency.”
“The orders were,” said the captain of the gate, as though he thought a word might be said here for his defence, “the orders were, my lord, that the tigers should be kept fierce and accustomed to killing.”
“Then, if you have obeyed orders, let me be the last to chide you. But it is my pleasure that this woman be respited, and I wish now to question her.”

===Chinese===

「大人，」那人说，「您若愿意，小人这就亲自下去同那两头洞虎走一遭，您可以在墙后观看着它们把我撕碎。」
「何必告诉我一件旧闻？」
「小人只想提醒大人记得自己的权柄；只想恳求大人的仁慈。」
「对这些可怜的囚徒，你的权柄已经显示过了；可就眼前剩下的这些看，尝过你多少仁慈的，没有几个。」
「上头的命令是，」守门队长说，仿佛觉得此处该替自己辩白一句，「命令是，大人，要把那几头虎养得凶悍，惯于杀生。」
「那么，你既奉了命令，便让责备你的最后一人是我罢。但这女人的死期且缓，这是我的意旨；现在我可有话要问她。」

===Original===

The man got to his feet again with obvious relief, though still bowing low.
“Then if my lord will honour me by sitting in my room that overlooks the outer gate, the favour will never be forgotten.”
“Show the way,” I said, and took the woman by the fingers, leading her gently. At the two ends of the circus the tigers prowled about on short chains, growling and muttering.
We passed through the door into the thickness of the outer wall, and the captain of the gate led us into his private chamber, a snug enough box overlooking the plain beyond the city. He lit a torch from his lamp and thrust it into a bracket on the wall, and bowing deeply and walking backwards, left us alone, closing the door in place behind him. He was an industrious fellow, this captain, to judge from the spoil with which his chamber was packed. There could have come very few traders in through that gate below without his levying a private tribute; and so, judging that most of his goods had been unlawfully come by, I had little qualm at making a selection. It was not decent that the woman, being an Atlantean, should go bereft of the dignity of clothes, as though she were a mere savage from Europe; and so I sought about amongst the captain’s spoil for garments that would be befitting.

===Chinese===

那人重新站起身来，明显松了一口气，腰却还躬得极低。
「那么，大人若肯赏光，到小人的房间里坐坐——那房间正俯临外门——这份恩典，小人永世不忘。」
「头前带路，」我说，便牵住那女人的手指，轻轻引着她走。圆场两头，虎在短链上逡巡，低吼着，咕哝着。
我们穿门走进外墙的厚度里，守门队长把我们引进他的私室——一间倒也齐整的小屋，俯临着城外的平原。他从灯上引燃一支火把，插进墙上的托架，深深躬着身子、倒退着出去，把门在我们身后掩回原位，留我们两个单独在一处。从满屋堆塞的赃物看，这位队长是位勤勉人。打底下那道门进来的客商，极少能躲过他抽一份私贡；因此，料想他的货物大半来得不干净，我也就没有多少不安，径自挑拣起来。这女人既是亚特兰蒂斯人，竟连衣装的体面都没有，像是个欧洲来的蛮女似的，那是不像话的；于是我便在队长的赃物里翻寻起合身的衣裳来。

===Original===

But, as I busied myself in this search for raiment, rummaging amongst the heaps and bales, with a hand and eye little skilled in such business, I heard a sound behind which caused me to turn my head, and there was the woman with a dagger she had picked from the floor, in the act of drawing it from the sheath.
She caught my eye and drew the weapon clear, but seeing that I made no advance towards her, or move to protect myself, waited where she was, and presently was took with a shuddering.
“Your designs seem somewhat of a riddle,” I said. “At first you wished to kill me from motives which you explained, and which I quite understood. It lay in my power next to confer some small benefit upon you, in consequence of which you are here, and not﻿—shall we say?﻿—yonder in the circus. Why you should desire now to kill the only man here who can set you completely free, and beyond these walls, is a thing it would gratify me much to learn. I say nothing of the trifle of ingratitude. Gratitude and ingratitude are of little weight here. There is some far greater in your mind.”

===Chinese===

可正当我忙着翻寻衣裳、在一堆堆一捆捆的货包里摸索——干这种营生，我的手和眼都不甚在行——忽听得身后一声响动，叫我回头：那女人手里正握着一柄从地上拾来的匕首，正把它从鞘里往外抽。
她撞上我的目光，把兵刃整个抽了出来；可她见我既不逼上前去，也不做护身的举动，便立在原地等着，不多时竟发起一阵颤抖。
「你的打算，倒像个谜，」我说。「起先你要杀我，缘由你自己讲过，我也全然明白。接下来又轮到我，有幸能施与你一点小小的好处，于是你此刻在这里，而不是——怎么说呢——在那边的圆场里。如今你为什么偏想杀掉此地唯一能放你彻底自由、送你出这道城墙的人，这件事若能领教，我倒是十分乐意。忘恩这点小事，我不提它。感恩也好，忘恩也好，在此处都无足轻重。你心里藏着的，是一件大得多的事。」

===Original===

She pressed a hand hard against her breasts. “You are Deucalion,” she gasped; “I heard you say it.”
“I am Deucalion. So far, I have known no reason to feel shame for my name.”
“And I come of those,” she cried, with a rising voice, “who bite against this city, because they have found their fate too intolerable with the land as it is ordered now. We heard of your coming from Yucatan. It was we who sent the fleet to take you at the entrance to the Gulf.”
“Your fleet gave us a pretty fight.”
“Oh, I know, I know. We had our watchers on the high land who brought us the tidings. We had an omen even before that. Where we lay with our army before the walls here, we saw great birds carrying off the slain to the mountains. But where the fleet failed, I saw a chance where I, a woman, might﻿—”

===Chinese===

她把一只手紧紧压在胸前。「你是德乌卡利翁，」她喘着气说，「我听见你自己说的。」
「我是德乌卡利翁。到目前为止，我还不曾知道我的名字有什么可羞之处。」
「而我，」她的声音扬了起来，「正出自那些噬咬这座城的人——因为这国土如今被治理成这般模样，我们的命已经苦得再受不了了。你从尤卡坦回来，我们得了信。派舰队到海湾入口去接拿你的，正是我们。」
「你们的舰队，倒叫我们打了一场漂亮仗。」
「哦，我知道，我知道。高地上有我们的人，消息是他们带来的。在那之前，我们还得了一个预兆。我们的军队屯在这城墙外时，亲眼看见大鸟把阵亡者衔往山里去。可舰队既然失了手，我瞧出一个机会——我一个女子，或许可以——」

===Original===

“Where you might succeed?” I sat me down on a pile of the captain’s stuffs. It seemed as if here at last that I should find a solution for many things. “You carry a name?” I asked.
“They call me Nais.”
“Ah,” I said, and signed to her to take the clothes that I had sought out. She was curiously like, so both my eyes and hearing said, to Ylga, the fan-girl of Phorenice, but as she had told me of no parentage I asked for none then. Still her talk alone let me know that she was bred of none of the common people, and I made up my mind towards definite understanding. “Nais,” I said, “you wish to kill me. At the same time I have no doubt you wish to live on yourself, if only to get credit from your people for what you have done. So here I will make a contract with you. Prove to me that my death is for Atlantis’ good, and I swear by our Lord the Sun to go out with you beyond the walls, where you can stab me and then get you gone. Or the﻿—”
“I will not be your slave.”
“I do not ask you for service. Or else, I wished to say, I shall live so long as the High Gods wish, and do my poor best for this country. And for you﻿—I shall set you free to do your best also. So now, I pray you, speak.”

===Chinese===

「——可以得手？」我在队长的一堆货包上坐下来。看来许多事情，我终究要在这里寻得一个解答了。「你有名字吧？」我问。
「他们叫我娜伊丝（Nais）。」
「啊，」我说，便示意她把我拣出来的衣裳拿去。她像极了——我的眼和耳都这样说——福伦尼刻那位执扇侍女伊尔加；只是她既未对我提起父母家世，我也就没有动问。然而单凭她这一番谈吐，我便知道她绝非出自寻常百姓人家，我心里便拿定了主意，要问个明白。「娜伊丝，」我说，「你要杀我。但同时我毫不怀疑，你自己也想活下去，哪怕只是为了向你的人领这份功劳。那么，我在这里同你立一个约。你若能向我证明，我的死有利于亚特兰蒂斯，我便对着我们的太阳神主起誓，随你出到城墙之外，任你刺死我，然后自去。要不然——」
「我不做你的奴隶。」
「我并不求你服侍。我是想说：要不然，我只消至上诸神愿意，我便活多久，为这国土尽我微薄的力量。至于你——我放你自由，也由你去尽你全力。那么现在，我求你了，说罢。」

## VII. The Biters of the Walls (Further Account) / 七　噬咬城墙者（续篇）

===Original===

“You will set me free,” she said, regarding me from under her brows, “without any further exactions or treaty?”

===Chinese===

「你肯放我自由，」她说，眼睛从眉睫底下打量着我，「不再另生勒索、另立约章？」

===Original===

“I will set you free exactly on those terms,” I answered, “unless indeed we here decide that it is better for Atlantis that I should die, in which case the freedom will be of your own taking.”

===Chinese===

「我必照着这些条件放你自由，」我答道，「除非我们当真在此议定，我死了对亚特兰蒂斯更好；果真那样，这自由便须由你自己动手去取。」

===Original===

“My lord plays a bold game.”

===Chinese===

「大人好大胆的一局棋。」

===Original===

“Tut, tut,” I said.

===Chinese===

「啧啧，」我说。

===Original===

“But I shall not hesitate to take the full of my bond, unless my theories are most clearly disproved to me.”

===Chinese===

「可除非有人把我的道理驳得明明白白，我决不迟疑，必把我契约上的权益取个十足。」

===Original===

“Tut,” I said, “you women, how you can play out the time needlessly. Show me sufficient cause, and you shall kill me where and how you please. Come, begin the accusation.”

===Chinese===

「啧，」我说，「你们这些女人啊，多么会白白耗磨光阴。给我看充分的缘由，你便可以在你乐意的地点、用你乐意的法子杀我。来罢，开始控诉。」

===Original===

“You are a tyrant.”

===Chinese===

「你是个暴君。」

===Original===

“At least I have not paraded my tyrannies in Atlantis these twenty years. Why, Nais, I did but land yesterday.”

===Chinese===

「至少这二十年来，我不曾在亚特兰蒂斯招摇过我的种种暴虐。哪里的话，娜伊丝，我昨天才上的岸。」

===Original===

“You will not deny you came back from Yucatan for a purpose.”

===Chinese===

「你不会否认，你从尤卡坦回来是抱着目的的。」

===Original===

“I came back because I was sent for. The Empress gives no reasons for her recalls. She states her will; and we who serve her obey without question.”

===Chinese===

「我回来，是因为承召。女皇的召还从不论理由。她宣示旨意；我们这些服事她的人，便无疑无误地遵从。」

===Original===

“Pah, I know that old dogma.”

===Chinese===

「呸，那套老教条我知道。」

===Original===

“If you discredit my poor honesty at the outset like this, I fear we shall not get far with our unravelling.”

===Chinese===

「你一开头就这般作践我这点可怜的诚实，我怕我们这桩解结的活计，是走不了多远的。」

===Original===

“My lord must be indeed simple,” said this strange woman scornfully, “if he is ignorant of what all Atlantis knows.”

===Chinese===

「大人若当真不知道这全亚特兰蒂斯人人皆知的事，」这古怪的女子讥诮道，「那他可真是天真得可以。」

===Original===

“Then simple you must write me down. Over yonder in Yucatan we were too well wrapped up in our own parochial needs and policies to have leisure to ponder much over the slim news which drifted out to us from Atlantis﻿—and, in truth, little enough came. By example, Phorenice (whose office be adored) is a great personage here at home; but over there in the colony we barely knew so much as her name. Here, since I have been ashore, I have seen many new wonders; I have been carried by a riding mammoth; I have sat at a banquet; but in what new policies there are afoot, I have yet to be schooled.”

===Chinese===

「那你就把我记作天真罢。在尤卡坦那边，我们太埋头于自己一方水土的需求与方略，没什么闲工夫去细想从亚特兰蒂斯漂传出去的那点稀薄消息——实话说，传来的也实在寥寥。譬如福伦尼刻（愿她的尊位受颂赞）在本土是位了不起的大人物；可在那边的殖民地，我们连她的名字都几乎不知道。我上岸以来，在这里见到了许多新的奇观；我坐过乘行的猛犸；赴过一场宴会；但如今在推行些什么新政，我还有待受教。」

===Original===

“Then, if truly you do not know it, let me repeat to you the common tale. Phorenice has tired of her unmated life.”

===Chinese===

「那么，你若果真不知，容我把这流传的说法讲给你听：福伦尼刻厌倦了她无偶的独居生活。」

===Original===

“Stay there. I will hear no word against the Empress.”

===Chinese===

「到此打住。我不听一句非议女皇的话。」

===Original===

“Pah, my lord, your scruples are most decorous. But I did no more than repeat what the Empress had made public by proclamation. She is minded to take to herself a husband, and nothing short of the best is good enough for Phorenice. One after another has been put up in turn as favourite﻿—and been found wanting. Oh, I tell you, we here in Atlantis have watched her courtship with jumping hearts. First it was this one here, then it was that one there; now it was this general just returned from a victory, and a day later he had been packed back to his camp, to give place to some dashing governor who had squeezed increased revenues from his province. But every ship that came from the West said that there was a stronger man than any of these in Yucatan, and at last the Empress changed the wording of her vow. ‘I’ll have Deucalion for my husband,’ said she, ‘and then we will see who can stand against my wishes.’ ”

===Chinese===

「呸，大人，你的顾虑真是体面得很。可我不过复述了女皇已用诏书公告天下的话。她有心给自己娶一位夫婿，而配得上福伦尼刻的，非顶好的人选不可。一个接一个被相继立作宠臣——又相继被验出成色不足。哦，我告诉你，我们这些亚特兰蒂斯人看她的求偶，心都是提到嗓子眼的。先是这边这一位，接着是那边那一位；这会儿是一位刚凯旋的将军，隔天他就被打发回了营地，让位给某位从行省榨出更多赋税的干练总督。可每一艘从西方来的船都说，尤卡坦有个比这些人都强的男子，于是女皇终于改了她誓愿的措辞。『我要德乌卡利翁做我的丈夫，』她说，『到那时看谁还敢违逆我的心愿。』」

===Original===

“The Empress (whose name be adored) can do as she pleases in such matters,” I said guardedly; “but that is beside the argument. I am here to know how it would be better for Atlantis that I should die?”

===Chinese===

「女皇（愿她的圣名受颂赞）在这类事情上尽可自行其是，」我措辞谨严地说，「可那离我们的论题远了。我坐在这里，是要知道：我的死何以会对亚特兰蒂斯更好？」

===Original===

“You know you are the strongest man in the kingdom.”

===Chinese===

「你自己知道，你是王国里最强的男子。」

===Original===

“It pleases you to say so.”

===Chinese===

「你乐意这么说。」

===Original===

“And Phorenice is the strongest woman.”

===Chinese===

「而福伦尼刻是最强的女子。」

===Original===

“That is beyond doubt.”

===Chinese===

「这毫无疑义。」

===Original===

“Why, then, if the Empress takes you in marriage, we shall be under a double tyranny. And her rule alone is more cruelly heavy than we can bear already.”

===Chinese===

「那么，女皇若招你为夫，我们岂不要受双重的暴政？何况单是她一人的统治，已经残酷沉重得叫我们不堪承受了。」

===Original===

“I pass no criticism on Phorenice’s rule. I have not seen it. But I crave your mercy, Nais, on the newcomer into this kingdom. I am strong, say you, and therefore I am a tyrant, say you. Now to me this sequence is faulty.”

===Chinese===

「福伦尼刻的治理，我不作褒贬。我还不曾见过。只是求你开恩，娜伊丝，宽待这王国新来的人。我强壮，你说；故而我是个暴君，你又说。可在我看来，这一串推理是有破绽的。」

===Original===

“Who should a strong man use strength for, if not for himself? And if for himself, why that spells tyranny. You will get all your heart’s desires, my lord, and you will forget that many a thousand of the common people will have to pay for them.”

===Chinese===

「一个强壮的汉子，使力气若不为自己，还为谁去？既为自己，那便分明是暴政了。大人，你心里的一切愿望都要得偿，你却会忘记：成千上万的平民，将不得不为这些愿望偿付代价。」

===Original===

“And this is all your accusation?”

===Chinese===

「这就是你全部的控诉？」

===Original===

“It seems to be black enough. I am one that has a compassion for my fellow-men, my lord, and because of that compassion you see me what I am today. There was a time, not long passed, when I slept as soft and ate as dainty as any in Atlantis.”

===Chinese===

「黑是够黑的了。我是个哀怜自己同胞的人，大人；正是那份哀怜，教你看见我落到今天这副模样。就在不久以前，我还睡得同亚特兰蒂斯随便哪个人一样软和，吃得一样精细。」

===Original===

I smiled. “Your speech told me that much from the first.”

===Chinese===

我微微一笑。「你的谈吐，从一开头就告诉我这许多了。」

===Original===

“Then I would I had cast the speech off, too, if that is also a livery of the tyrant’s class. But I tell you I saw all the oppression myself from the oppressor’s side. I was high in Phorenice’s favour then.”

===Chinese===

「那我倒愿把这份谈吐也一并扔掉，倘若它也是暴君阶层的一袭号衣。可我告诉你，那些欺压，我是从欺压者那一边亲眼看全的。那时我正身处福伦尼刻的宠信之中。」

===Original===

“That, too, is easy of credence. Ylga is the fan-girl to the Empress now, and second lady in the kingdom, and those who have seen Ylga could make an easy guess at the parentage of Nais.”

===Chinese===

「这也容易取信。伊尔加如今是女皇的执扇侍女，王国的第二贵妇；见过伊尔加的人，都不难猜到娜伊丝的出身来历。」

===Original===

“We were the daughters of one birth; but I do not count with either Zaemon or Ylga now. Ylga is the creature of Phorenice, and Phorenice would have all the people of Atlantis slaves and in chains, so that she might crush them the easier. And as for Zaemon, he is no friend of Phorenice’s; he fights with brain and soul to drag the old authority to those on the Sacred Mountain; and that, if it come down on us again, would only be the exchange of one form of slavery for another.”

===Chinese===

「我们原是一胎同生的女儿；可如今，泽蒙也罢，伊尔加也罢，我都不相与了。伊尔加是福伦尼刻的造物；而福伦尼刻恨不得叫亚特兰蒂斯的万民尽为奴隶、尽上锁链，好教她碾压起来愈加省力。至于泽蒙，他也不是福伦尼刻的朋友；他以脑与魂力战，要把旧日的权柄夺还给圣山上那些人；那权柄若再落到我们头上，也不过是以一种奴役换另一种奴役罢了。」

===Original===

“It seems to me you bite at all authority.”

===Chinese===

「照我看来，你是逢着任何权柄都要咬上一口。」

===Original===

“In fact,” she said simply, “I do. I have seen too much of it.”

===Chinese===

「事实上，」她坦然道，「我确实如此。这种东西，我见得太多了。」

===Original===

“And so you think a rule of no-rule would be best for the country?”

===Chinese===

「于是你认为，一种『无治之治』于这国家最好？」

===Original===

“You have put it plainly in words for me. That is my creed today. That is the creed of all those yonder, who sit in the camp and besiege this city. And we number on our side, now, all in Atlantis save those in the city and a handful on the priests’ Mountain.”

===Chinese===

「你替我用明明白白的话说出来了。这便是我今日的信条。也就是那边那些坐拥营垒、围困此城的人，人人共有的信条。而今，全亚特兰蒂斯——除了城中之人，和圣山上那一小撮祭司——都站在我们这一边。」

===Original===

I shook my head. “A creed of desperation, if you like, Nais, but, believe me, a silly creed. Since man was born out of the quakings and the fevers of this earth, and picked his way amongst the cooler-places, he has been dependent always on his fellow-men. And where two are congregated together, one must be chief, and order how matters are to be governed﻿—at least, I speak of men who have a wish to be higher than the beasts. Have you ever set foot in Europe?”

===Chinese===

我摇了摇头。「这信条，你尽管叫它绝望者的信条，娜伊丝，可是相信我，它是个愚蠢的信条。自从人从大地的震颤与高热中降生，在较为阴凉的地带间择路而行，他就无时不依赖着自己的同类。而哪里有两个人聚在一处，哪里就必须有一人为首，来定夺诸事如何治理——至少，我说的是那些有心高出兽类一等的人。你可曾踏足欧洲？」

===Original===

“No.”

===Chinese===

「没有。」

===Original===

“I have. Years back I sailed there, gathering slaves. What did I see? A country without rule or order. Tyrants they were, to be sure, but they were the beasts. The men and the women were the rudest savages, knowing nothing of the arts, dressing in skins and uncleanness, harbouring in caves and the treetops. The beasts roamed about where they would, and hunted them unchecked.”

===Chinese===

「我去过。多年以前，我航行到那里去收掠奴隶。我见到了什么？一个没有统御、没有秩序的国度。暴君么，倒确乎是有的，可那暴君便是野兽。男女都是最粗鄙的蛮人，于诸般技艺一无所知，以兽皮与垢污蔽体，栖身在洞穴与树顶之间。野兽随意四处游荡，猎食他们，无人禁遏。」

===Original===

“Still, they fought you for their liberty?”

===Chinese===

「尽管如此，他们还是为自由同你们打过仗？」

===Original===

“Never once. They knew how disastrous was their masterless freedom. Even to their dull, savage brains it was a sure thing that no slavery could be worse; and to that state you, and your friends, and your theories, will reduce Atlantis, if you get the upper hand. But, then, to argue in a circle, you will never get it. For to conquer, you must set up leaders, and once you have set them up, you will never pull them down again.”

===Chinese===

「一回也没有。他们知道，那无主的自由是何等的灾难。就连他们迟钝的蛮人头脑里，也笃定着一件事：再没有哪种奴役比这更坏了。而你们、你们的朋友、你们的那套道理，一旦得了上风，便要把亚特兰蒂斯也糟蹋到那个地步。不过——说句循环往复的话——你们是永远得不了上风的。因为要制胜，就必须拥立首领；而首领一旦立起，你们便再也休想把他们扳倒了。」

===Original===

“Aye,” she said with a sigh, “there is truth in that last.”

===Chinese===

「唉，」她叹道，「末了那句，倒有几分道理。」

===Original===

The torch had filled the captain’s room with a resinous smoke, but the flame was growing pale. Dawn was coming in greyly through a slender arrow-slit, and with it ever and again the glow from some mountain out of sight, which was shooting forth spasmodic bursts of fire. With it also were mutterings of distant falling rocks, and sullen tremblings, which had endured all the night through, and I judged that earth was in one of her quaking moods, and would probably during the forthcoming day offer us some chastening discomforts.

===Chinese===

火把把树脂的浓烟灌满了守城队长的房间，火苗却渐渐变得苍白。黎明正从一道细窄的箭缝灰蒙蒙地透进来，随它同来的，还有一座看不见的山头时或吐出的红光——那山正一阵一阵地喷着火。伴随黎明的，还有远处岩崩的隆隆声和沉郁的震动，这些已彻夜不休。我断定，大地又犯了她震颤的性子，多半要在接下来的一天里，降给我们几样惩戒性的苦楚。

===Original===

On this account, perhaps, my senses were stilled to certain evidences which would otherwise have given me a suspicion; and also, there is no denying that my general wakefulness was sapped by another matter. This woman, Nais, interested me vastly out of the common; the mere presence of her seemed to warm the organs of my interior; and whilst she was there, all my thoughts and senses were present in the room of the captain of the gate in which we sat.

===Chinese===

或者正因这个缘故，我的感官对某些迹象竟麻木了——换了别时，那些迹象必叫我起疑；而且无可讳言，我通体的警醒，也叫另一桩事消解了。这个女子，娜伊丝，唤起了我远超寻常的兴趣；单是她人在此间，似乎便把我腹中的脏腑都烘得暖暖的；她在的时候，我的全部心思与感官，都齐齐守在我们同坐的这间守城队长的屋子里。

===Original===

But of a sudden the floor of the chamber rocked and fell away beneath me, and in a tumult of dust, and litter, and bales of the captain’s plunder, I fell down (still seated on the flagstone) into a pit which had been digged beneath it. With the violence of the descent, and the flutter of all these articles about my head, I was in no condition for immediate action; and whilst I was still half-stunned by the shock, and long before I could get my eyes into service again, I had been seized, and bound, and half-strangled with a noose of hide. Voices were raised that I should be despatched at once out of the way; but one in authority cried out that, killing me at leisure, and as a prisoner, promised more genteel sport; and so I was thrust down on the floor, whilst a whole army of men trod in over me to the attack.

===Chinese===

可是倏忽之间，那房间的地面在我身下一晃、塌陷了下去；在一团尘土、杂物与守城队长成包掠获物的纷乱里，我（人还坐在那方石板上）跌进了底下早掘好的一个坑里。下坠之势猛恶，这些东西又在我头顶纷飞乱舞，我一时全然无力举措；我尚被这一撞震得半昏，眼睛也远不曾重新当差，就已遭人擒住、捆定，又被一根皮索勒得半窒息。有人扬声主张，该即刻把我打发掉了事；却有一个有权柄的高声喊道，把我当囚徒、从容地处死，才更有几分雅致的乐趣；于是我给按倒在地上，整整一支大军从我身上踏过，涌去攻杀。

===Original===

What had happened was clear to me now, though I was powerless to do anything in hindrance. The rebels with more craft than anyone had credited to them, had driven a galley from their camp under the ground, intending so to make an entrance into the heart of the city. In their clumsy ignorance, and having no one of sufficient talent in mensuration, they had bungled sadly both in direction and length, and so had ended their burrow under this chamber of the captain of the gate. The great flagstone in its fall had, it appeared, crushed four of them to death, but these were little noticed or lamented. Life was to them a bauble of the slenderest price, and a horde of others pressed through the opening, lusting for the fight, and recking nothing of their risks and perils.

===Chinese===

此刻出了什么事，我心里已经清楚，只是无力作分毫阻拦罢了。这些叛党，比谁归给他们的评价都更工于心计，竟从他们的大营往地下开出了一条坑道，指望就此直入城的心脏。怎奈他们手脚拙劣、冥顽无知，度量之才又无一人够格，方向和长短双双弄糟得厉害，那条地洞便恰恰收在守城队长的这间房底下。那方大石板塌落时，看情形压毙了他们四个人，可这些人几乎无人理会，也无人哀恸。性命于他们不过是一件最不值钱的玩物，另有一大群人仍从那豁口往里挤，嗜杀心炽，全不把自己的险厄放在心上。

===Original===

Half-choked by the foul air of the galley, and trodden on by this great procession of feet, it was little enough I could do to help my immediate self much less the more distant city. But when the chief mass of the attackers had passed through, and there came only here and there one eager to take his share at storming the gate, a couple of fellows plucked me up out of the mud on the floor, and began dragging me down through the stinking darkness of the galley towards the pit that gave it entrance.

===Chinese===

坑道里的浊气呛得我半死，又被这浩浩荡荡一队脚掌践踏而过，我能照应眼前自身的本已无几，更遑论远处那座城。但等攻击者的主体过去了，只余三三两两的人赶来夺门、要分上一份，才有两个汉子把我从地上的泥泞里拖起，拽着我穿过坑道发臭的黑暗，朝它进口的竖坑走去。

===Original===

Twenty times we were jostled by others hastening to the attack, either from hunger for fight, or from appetite for what they could steal. But we came to the open at last, and half-suffocated though I was, I contrived to do obeisance, and say aloud the prescribed prayer to the most High Gods in gratitude for the fresh, sweet air which They had provided.

===Chinese===

一路上，我们叫旁的人挤撞了不下二十回——他们或出于嗜战的饥渴，或贪图能顺手偷摸些什么。但我们终究到了露天。我尽管窒息得半死，仍勉力躬身行礼，高声诵出那规定的祷文，向最高的诸神致谢，谢他们赐下这清新甜美的空气。

===Original===

Our Lord the Sun was on the verge of rising for His day, and all things were plainly shown. Before me were the monstrous walls of the capital, with the heads of its pyramids and higher buildings showing above them. And on the walls, the sentries walked calmly their appointed paces, or took shelter against arrows in the casemates provided for them.

===Chinese===

太阳神主正要升起来开始他的一日，万物已历历可辨。我面前是都城那些骇人的城墙，城中的金字塔与更高的建筑，顶儿都探出在墙头之上。墙上，哨兵们安闲地踱着指定的步数，或躲进为他们备下的掩蔽室中避箭。

===Original===

The din of fighting within the gate rose high into the air, and the heavy roaring of the cave-tigers told that they too were taking their share of the melee. But the massive stonework of the walls hid all the actual engagement from our view, and which party was getting the upper hand we could not even guess. But the sounds told how tight a fight was being hammered out in those narrow boundaries, and my veins tingled to be once more back at the old trade, and to be doing my share.

===Chinese===

门内厮杀的喧嚣高高腾入空中，洞虎沉重的吼声告诉人：这场混战里也有它们的一份。但城墙厚重的石工，把实际的接战全然遮蔽在我们视线之外；哪一方占了上风，我们连猜也无处猜起。可那些声浪足以说明，在那几重狭窄的界限之内，一场何等吃紧的恶战正一锤一锤锻打出来；我的血管里针刺般发麻发烫，只盼着重回旧日的行当，去尽自己的一份。

===Original===

But there was no chivalry about the fellows who held me by my bonds. They thrust me into a small temple near by, which once had been a fane in much favour with travellers, who wished to show gratitude for the safe journey to the capital, but which now was robbed and ruined, and they swung to the stone entrance gate and barred it, leaving me to commune with myself. Presently, they told me, I should be put to death by torments. Well, this seemed to be the new custom of Atlantis, and I should have to endure it as best I could. The High Gods, it appeared, had no further use for my services in Atlantis, and I was not in the mood then to bite very much at their decision. What I had seen of the country since my return had not enamoured me very much with its new conditions.

===Chinese===

可是，攥着我绑绳的那两个家伙，身上寻不出半分骑士气。他们把我塞进近旁一座小庙。那庙从前极受行旅的青眼——都是些平安抵京、要谢恩的人——如今却被劫掠一空、颓败不堪。他们推拢石造的庙门，下了闩，留下我独自与自己对谈。他们说，不多时，我便要受尽酷刑而死。罢罢，这似乎便是亚特兰蒂斯的新风俗，我也只好尽力捱它一遭。看来，至上诸神在亚特兰蒂斯已用不着我效力了；而我彼时的心绪，也不很想去咬啃他们这番裁断。自归来之日起，我所见的这片国土，并未教我恋上它的新气象分毫。

===Original===

The little temple in which I was gaoled had been robbed and despoiled of all its furnishments. But the light-slits, where at certain hours of the day the rays of our Lord the Sun had fallen upon the image of the God, before this had been taken away, gave me vantage places from which I could see over the camp of these rebel besiegers, and a dreary prospect it was. The people seemed to have shucked off the culture of centuries in as many months, and to have gone back for the most part to sheer brutishness. The majority harboured on the bare ground. Few owned shelter, and these were merely bowers of mud and branches.

===Chinese===

囚禁我的这座小庙，一切陈设早被劫夺净尽。但那几道透光的狭缝——从前，一日里几个时辰，太阳神主的光线曾透过它们落在神像上，如今神像是被搬走了——倒给了我几处高踞的望点，从那里可以望尽这些叛军围城者的大营。那是何等荒凉的一幅景象。这些人仿佛只消几个月，便把几百年的文教蜕了个干净，大半已退回十足的兽性。多数人就露宿在光秃秃的地面上。有棚遮身的寥寥无几，且不过是泥浆树枝搭起的窝棚。

===Original===

They fought and quarrelled amongst themselves for food, eating their meat raw, and their grain (when they had it) unground. Many who passed my vision I saw were even gnawing the soft inside of tree bark.

===Chinese===

他们为着吃食自相殴斗吵嚷；肉是生吃的，谷子（若有时）也不磨便吞。我看见许多打我眼前经过的人，甚至在啃嚼树皮的软瓤。

===Original===

The dead lay where they fell. The sick and the wounded found no hand to tend them. Great man-eating birds hovered about the camp or skulked about, heavy with gorging, amongst the hovels, and no one had public spirit enough to give them battle. The stink of the place rose up to heaven as a foul incense inviting a pestilence. There was no order, no trace of strong command anywhere. With three hundred well-disciplined troops it seemed to me that I could have sent those poor desperate hordes flying in panic to the forest.

===Chinese===

死者倒在哪里就躺在哪里。病人伤者寻不着一只手来照拂。巨大的食人鸟在营地上空盘旋，或饱啖之后沉甸甸地在窝棚间逡巡，竟无一人有几分公德，肯去同它们见一阵仗。这地方的恶臭直冲上天，像一股秽恶的熏香，招引着瘟疫。处处没有秩序，没有一丝强干号令的踪影。依我看，只消三百名纪律严明的兵卒，我便能把那些可怜绝望的乌合之众杀得魂飞魄散、抱头逃进林子里去。

===Original===

However, there was no very lengthy space of time granted me for thinking out the policy of this matter to any great depth. The attack on the gate had been delivered with suddenness; the repulse was not slow. Of what desperate fighting took place in the galleries, and in the circus between the two sets of gates, the detail will never be told in full.

===Chinese===

不过，容我把这桩事体的方略细细想深的时间，却没有多长。对城门的攻击发难得突然；击退得也不迟缓。至于那几条廊道里、两重城门之间的圆场里，究竟有过怎样拼死的搏斗，其细节将永远无人能道其全了。

===Original===

At the first alarm the great cave-tigers were set loose, and these raged impartially against keeper and foe. Of those that went in through the tunnel, not one in ten returned, and there were few of these but what carried a bloody wound. Some, with the ruling passion still strong in them, bore back plunder; one trailed along with him the head of the captain of the gate; and amongst them they dragged out two of the warders who were wounded, and whom revenge had urged them to take as prisoners.

===Chinese===

头一声警响起，那两头巨大的洞虎便被放开，它们不分看管者与仇家，一概狂怒地扑咬。经坑道进去的人，十个里回来的不到一个；回来的这些人，又少有不带着血淋淋的创口。有几个嗜杀之念犹炽，还携了掠获物回来；有一个拖曳着守城队长的首级；他们中间又拖出两个受伤的看守——是复仇之心撺掇他们掳来的俘虏。

===Original===

Over these two last a hubbub now arose, that seemed likely to boil over into blows. Every voice shouted out for them what he thought the most repulsive fate. Some were for burning, some for skinning, some for impaling, some for other things: my flesh crept as I heard their ravenous yells. Those that had been to the trouble of making them captive were still breathless from the fight, and were readily thrust aside; and it seemed to me that the poor wretches would be hustled into death before any definite fate was agreed upon, which all would pass as sufficiently terrific. Never had I seen such a disorderly tumult, never such a leaderless mob. But, as always has happened, and always will, the stronger men by dint of louder voices and more vigorous shoulders got their plans agreed to at last, and the others perforce had to give way.

===Chinese===

单为这末后两人，此刻便掀起一场喧嚣，眼看就要沸成拳脚。每一条喉咙都替他们嚷出自己心目中最不堪的死法：有的主张烧，有的主张剥皮，有的主张穿刺，有的主张别的名堂：我听着他们那贪馋的嚎叫，皮肉不由得悚悚发麻。那些费了力气将他们生擒的人，兀自喘息未定，就被轻易挤到一旁；依我看，不等众人议定哪种分明的结局——随便哪种，大家都会认作够惨——那两个可怜虫就要在推搡喧嚣里被塞进死亡了。我从未见过这般纷乱的骚动，从未见过这般无头的暴众。但，正如历来如此、将来也永远如此的那样，更强悍的汉子们凭着更响的嗓门、更硬的肩膀，终究叫众人认可了他们的方案，其余的也只得屈从让路。

===Original===

A band of them set off running, and presently returned at snails’ pace, dragging with them (with many squeals from ungreased wheels) one of those huge war engines with which besiegers are wont to throw great stones and other missiles into the cities they sit down against. They ran it up just beyond bowshot of the walls, and clamped it firmly down with stakes and ropes to the earth. Then setting their lean arms to the windlasses, they drew back the great tree which formed the spring till its tethering place reached the ground, and in the cradle at its head they placed one of the prisoners, bound helplessly, so that he could not throw himself over the side.

===Chinese===

一小队人跑步去了，不多时又蜗牛般爬回来，身后拖着一架巨大的攻城重械（车轮不曾上油，一路吱嘎尖叫）——围城者惯用这等器械，朝他们扎营久困的城池抛掷巨石与别的弹丸。他们把它推到城墙外弓箭恰好不及的地方，用木桩和绳索把它牢牢钉锁在地上。随后他们把精瘦的胳膊压上绞盘，把充作弹簧的那株大树扳了回来，直到系索之处触到地面；又把一个囚徒放进树头顶端的摇篮里，捆得妥妥帖帖，教他无从翻身跃出。

===Original===

Then the rude, savage, skin-clad mob stood back, and one who had appointed himself engineer knocked back the catch that held the great spring in place.

===Chinese===

随后，那粗野的、披着兽皮的暴众退开，一个自封的机师叩开了扣住大弹簧的搭扣。

===Original===

With a whir and a twang the elastic wood flung upwards, and the bound man was shot away from its tip with the speed of a lightning flash. He sang through the air, spinning over and over with inconceivable rapidity, and the great crowd of rebels held their breath in silence as they watched. He passed high above the city wall, a tiny mannikin in the distance now, and then the trajectory of his flight began to lower. The spike of a new-built pyramid lay in the path of his terrific flight, and he struck it with a thud whose sound floated out to us afterwards, and then he toppled down out of our sight, leaving a red stain on the whiteness of the stone as he fell.

===Chinese===

呼的一声、弦的一响，那弹性的大木猛然向上甩去，被缚的男子从木梢头被射了出去，疾若闪电。他尖啸着掠过天空，以不可思议的迅疾翻滚不休，大群的叛军屏息敛声地看着。他高高地越过城墙，此刻在远处已成一个小小的人形，随后他飞行的弧线开始低垂。一座新建金字塔的塔尖，正横在他这骇人飞程的路径上；他砰地撞了上去，那一声闷响事后才飘送到我们耳边；接着他便翻跌出去，没了踪影，坠落时在白石的尖顶上留下一道红痕。

===Original===

With a roar the crowd acknowledged the success of their device, and bellowed out insults to Phorenice, and insults to the Gods: a poor frantic crowd they showed themselves. And then with ravening shouts, they fell upon the other captive warder, binding him also into a compact helpless missile, and meanwhile getting the engine in gear again for another shot.

===Chinese===

人群轰然一声，庆贺他们的器械得售，又朝福伦尼刻吼出辱骂，朝诸神吼出辱骂：他们显露的，是一副何等可卑、何等癫狂的众生相。随后，他们挟着贪馋的呼喊，扑向另一个被俘的看守，也把他捆作一件严严实实、无从挣扎的弹丸，一面又给那器械上好弦，预备再发一矢。

===Original===

But for my part I saw nothing of this disgusting scene. I heard the bolt grate stealthily against the door of the little temple in which I was imprisoned, and was minded to give these brutish rebels somewhat of a surprise. I had rid myself of my bonds handily enough; I had rubbed my limbs to that perfect suppleness which is always desirable before a fight; and I had planned to rush out so soon as the door was swung, and kill those that came first with fist blows on the brow and chin.

===Chinese===

至于我，这可憎的一幕是一星半点也没有看见。我听见门闩在我被囚小庙的门上窸窸窣窣地磨响，便存心要给这些野蛮的叛党一点小小的惊奇。我早已利利落落脱却了绑缚；我已把四肢揉搓到那种临斗之前永远相宜的十足柔韧；我定下的方略是：门一荡开便冲出去，用拳头猛击眉心与下颏，先结果打头进来的几个。

===Original===

They had not suspected my name, it was clear, for my stature and garb were nothing out of the ordinary; but if my bodily strength and fighting power had been sufficient to raise me to a viceroyalty like that of Yucatan, and let me endure alive in that government throughout twenty hard-battling years, why, it was likely that this rabble of savages would see something that was new and admirable in the practice of arms before the crude weight of their numbers could drag me down. Nay, I did not even despair of winning free altogether. I must find me a weapon from those that came up to battle, with which I could write worthy signatures, and I must attempt no standing fights. Gods! but what a glow the prospect did send through me as I stood there waiting.

===Chinese===

他们显然不曾疑及我的名头，因为我的身量与装束都毫无出奇之处；可是，我的膂力与战力，既足以把我抬到尤卡坦那样一个总督的高位，又容我在那任上完完整整捱过二十个鏖战之年，那么，多半不等这群蛮子拿他们生蛮的人数的粗重分量把我压倒，他们便要领教些武艺之中新奇可佩的东西了。不，我甚至并不绝望于赢得一个全身而退。我须从上前来交手的人手里夺一件兵器，好拿它签下无愧的署名；我也须避免任何站定了的缠斗。诸神啊！我立在那里等候的时候，这前景送进我周身的，是怎样的一股热流！

===Original===

A vainer man, writing history, might have said that always, before everything else, he held in mind the greater interests before the less. But for me﻿—I prefer to be honest, and own myself human. In my glee at that forthcoming fight﻿—which promised to be the greatest and most furious I had known in all a long life of battling﻿—I will confess that Atlantis and her differing policies were clean forgot. I should go out an unknown man from the little cell of a temple, I should do my work, and then, whether I took freedom with me, or whether I came down at last myself on a pile of slain, these people would guess without being told the name, that here was Deucalion. Gods! what a fight we would have made!

===Chinese===

换一个更虚荣的人来写史，也许会说：他在任何时候、在任何事之前，心里先存着的，都是大利先于小利。至于我——我宁愿诚实，自认是个凡人。那场眼面前要来的恶斗——有望成为我漫长一生鏖战中经历过的最盛大、最狂烈的一场——使我那般欢腾，我招认：亚特兰蒂斯和她那纷纷的方略，都被我忘了个干净。我该当一个无名之人，从这庙宇的小小一间里走出去；我该做完我的活计；然后，不论我携着自由脱身，还是我自己末了也倒在一堆尸骸之上，这些人不必有人通名，也会猜到：这是德乌卡利翁。诸神啊！那将是怎样的一场恶斗！

===Original===

But the door did not open wide to give me space for my first rush. It creaked gratingly outwards on its pivots, and a slim hand and a white arm slipped inside, beckoning me to quietude. Here was some woman. The door creaked wider, and she came inside.

===Chinese===

可是那门并没有大大敞开，容我施展头一轮冲杀。它在枢轴上轧轧地朝外吱呀，一只纤手、一条白臂先溜了进来，招呼我缄默。来的是个女子。门又轧轧地开宽了些，她走了进来。

===Original===

“Nais,” I said.

===Chinese===

「娜伊丝，」我说。

===Original===

“Silence, or they will hear you, and remember. At present those who brought you here are killed, and unless by chance someone blunders into this robbed shrine, you will not be found.”

===Chinese===

「噤声，不然他们会听见你、记下你。此刻，把你掳到此处来的人都已死了；除非碰巧有人误撞进这座被劫的庙宇，你是不会被发觉的。」

===Original===

“Then, if that is so, let me go out and walk amongst these people as one of themselves.”

===Chinese===

「那么，既然如此，就容我出去，走到这些人中间，作为他们当中的一人。」

===Original===

She shook her head.

===Chinese===

她摇了摇头。

===Original===

“But, Nais, I am not known here. I am merely a man in very plain and mud-stained robe. I should be in no ways remarkable.”

===Chinese===

「可是，娜伊丝，这里无人识得我。我不过是个穿一身极朴素、溅了泥的袍子的男人。断不至于惹人注目。」

===Original===

A smile twitched her face. “My lord,” she said, “wears no beard; and his is the only clean chin in the camp.”

===Chinese===

一丝笑意牵动了她的脸。「大人，」她说，「面上不曾留须；他这下巴，是全营中唯一干净的一个。」

===Original===

I joined in her laugh. “A pest on my want of foppishness then. But I am forgetting somewhat. It comes to my mind that we still have unfinished that small discussion of ours concerning the length of my poor life. Have you decided to cut it off from risk of further mischief, or do you propose to give me further span?”

===Chinese===

我也随着她笑。「那就叫我这份不知修饰遭瘟罢。不过我倒忘了些事。我想起来，关于我这条贱命的长短，我们那场小小的辩论还没了呢。你可已定意要将它截去、以免再酿祸殃，还是打算再赐我一段寿数？」

===Original===

She turned to me with a look of sharp distress. “My lord,” she said, “I would have you forget that silly talk of mine. This last two hours I thought you were dead in real truth.”

===Chinese===

她转向我，脸上带着一阵尖锐的痛楚。「大人，」她说，「我愿你忘了我那些痴话。这两个钟头里，我只当你当真死了。」

===Original===

“And you were not relieved?”

===Chinese===

「而你没有松一口气？」

===Original===

“I felt that the only man was gone out of the world﻿—I mean, my lord, the only man who can save Atlantis.”

===Chinese===

「我只觉得，世上唯一的男子去了——我是说，大人，那唯一能救亚特兰蒂斯的男子。」

===Original===

“Your words give me a confidence. Then you would have me go back and become husband to Phorenice?”

===Chinese===

「你这话给了我信心。那么，你愿意我回去，做福伦尼刻的夫婿？」

===Original===

“If there is no other way.”

===Chinese===

「若是没有别的路。」

===Original===

“I warn you I shall do that, if she still so desires it, and if it seems to me that that course will be best. This is no hour for private likings or dislikings.”

===Chinese===

「我警告你，只要她仍这般属意，只要在我看来那条路子最为上策，我便要那样做。此刻不是讲究私人爱憎的时辰。」

===Original===

“I know it,” she said, “I feel it. I have no heart now, save only for Atlantis. I have schooled myself once more to that.”

===Chinese===

「我知道，」她说，「我觉得出。如今我这一颗心，除了亚特兰蒂斯再无别的。我又一次把自己调教到这一步了。」

===Original===

“And at present I am in this lone little box of a temple. A minute ago, before you came, I had promised myself a pretty enough fight to signalise my changing of abode.”

===Chinese===

「而眼下，我正待在这孤零零、匣子般的小庙里。方才，你来之前，我还许下了自己一场相当痛快的恶斗，好纪念这次乔迁之喜。」

===Original===

“There must be nothing of that. I will not have these poor people slaughtered unnecessarily. Nor do I wish to see my lord exposed to a hopeless risk. This poor place, such as it is, has been given to me as an abode, and, if my lord can remain decorously till nightfall in a maiden’s chamber, he may at least be sure of quietude. I am a person,” she added simply, “that in this camp has some respect. When darkness comes, I will take my lord down to the sea and a boat, and so he may come with ease to the harbour and the water-gate.”

===Chinese===

「那是万万不可的。我不能让这些可怜人平白遭屠戮，也不愿看着大人去冒那无望的险。这个寒酸地方，虽则如此，已拨给我作居所了；大人若能在一间闺阁里规矩地待到夜幕降临，至少稳可得一份清静。我这个人，」她坦然添了一句，「在这营中颇受几分敬重。天黑之后，我便领大人下到海边，登上一条小船，大人便可以从从容容，抵达港口与水门（water-gate）。」

## VIII. The Preacher from the Mountains / 八　山中来的布道者

===Original===

It was long enough since I had found leisure for a parcel of sleep, and so during the larger part of that day I am free to confess that I slumbered soundly, Nais watching me. Night fell, and still we remained within the privacy of the temple. It was our plan that I should stay there till the camp slept, and so I should have more chance of reaching the sea without disturbance.

===Chinese===

我已许久不曾得闲睡上一个囫囵觉了，所以那一天的大半工夫，我坦白承认，我睡得又沉又酣，娜伊丝在一旁守着我。夜幕落下，我们仍留在庙里那处无人打搅的僻静地。我们商定的方略是：我在那里待到全营睡熟，这样我去海边便更有望不受搅扰。

===Original===

The night came down wet, with a drizzle of rain, and through the slits in the temple walls we could see the many fires in the camp well cared for, the men and women in skins and rags toasting before them, with steam rising as the heat fought with their wetness. Folk seated in discomfort like this are proverbially alert and cruel in the temper, and Nais frowned as she looked on the inclemency of the weather.

===Chinese===

这一夜带着蒙蒙细雨湿漉漉地压了下来。透过庙墙上的狭缝，我们望得见营中一堆堆侍弄得妥帖的篝火，披兽皮、着破衣的男男女女在火前烘烤，热气同他们身上的湿气相争，蒸腾起阵阵白雾。像这样坐得难受的人，脾气向来是出了名的警醒刻毒；娜伊丝望着这恶劣的天气，不由皱起了眉。

===Original===

“A fine night,” she said, “and I would have sent my lord back to the city without a soul here being the wiser; but in this chill, people sleep sourly. We must wait till the hour drugs them sounder.”

===Chinese===

「好一个夜晚，」她说，「我原想就此送大人回城，不叫这里的一个人知觉；可在这样的寒气里，人人睡得浮躁。我们只得等时辰把他们药得更沉些。」

===Original===

And so we waited, sitting there together on that pavement so long unkissed by worshippers, and it was little enough we said aloud. But there can be good companionship without sentences of talk.

===Chinese===

于是我们便等着，一同坐在那片久已无信徒亲吻的石铺地上，出声说出的话寥寥无几。但没有成句的言谈，也照样可以有很好的相伴。

===Original===

But as the hours drew on, the night began to grow less quiet. From the distance someone began to blow on a horn or a shell, sending forth a harsh raucous note incessantly. The sound came nearer, as we could tell from its growing loudness, and the voices of those by the fires made themselves heard, railing at the blower for his disturbance. And presently it became stationary, and standing up we could see through the slits in the walls the people of the camp rousing up from their uneasy rest, and clustering together round one who stood and talked to them from the pedestal of a war engine.

===Chinese===

但随着时辰推移，夜开始变得不那么安静了。远处有人吹响了一支号角或是一只螺壳，不住地送出一声声粗厉嘶哑的鸣响。那声音越来越近——我们听得出它愈来愈响——火堆旁的人们的嗓门也嚷了开来，咒骂那吹号人搅扰。不多时，那声音停驻下来；我们站起身，透过墙上的狭缝，望见营中的人们从不安的歇息里被搅起，正围拢聚在一个站在攻城重械座架上向他们说话的人四周。

===Original===

What he was declaiming upon we could not hear, and our curiosity on the matter was not keen. Given that all who did not sleep went to weary themselves with this fellow, as Nais whispered, it would be simple for me to make an exit in the opposite direction.

===Chinese===

他在慷慨陈词些什么，我们听不见，我们对这上头的好奇心也不热切。娜伊丝低声说，既然没睡的人都去同这人耗神了，我朝相反方向脱身，原是轻而易举的事。

===Original===

But here we were reckoning without the inevitable busybody. A dozen pairs of feet splashing through the wet came up to the side of the little temple, and cried loudly that Nais should join the audience. She had eloquence of tongue, it appeared, and they feared lest this speaker who had taken his stand on the war engine should make schisms amongst their ranks unless some skilled person stood up also to refute his arguments.

===Chinese===

可我们这一盘算，偏偏漏算了那少不了的爱管闲事之人。十几双脚踩着湿泥、溅着水花，来到小庙旁边，大声叫嚷着要娜伊丝也去加入听众。看来她生有一条利口；他们生怕那站在攻城重械上开口的人，会在他们队伍里搅出分裂，除非有个能言的人也站起来驳倒他的论调。

===Original===

Here, then, it seemed to me that I must be elbowed into my skirmish by the most unexpected of chances, but Nais was firmly minded that there should be no fight, if courage on her part could turn it. “Come out with me,” she whispered, “and keep distant from the light of the fires.”

===Chinese===

这样一来，依我看，我竟要被这最意想不到的机缘，硬挤进我那场厮杀里去了；可娜伊丝铁了心：只要她一方面的胆气能扭转，便绝不容有一战。「随我出来，」她悄声道，「离火光远远的。」

===Original===

“But how explain my being here?”

===Chinese===

「可怎么解释我在这儿的事？」

===Original===

“There is no reason to explain anything,” she said bitterly. “They will take you for my lover. There is nothing remarkable in that: it is the mode here. But oh, why did not the Gods make you wear a beard, and curl it, even as other men? Then you could have been gone and safe these two hours.”

===Chinese===

「什么都不用解释，」她辛酸地说。「他们会拿你当我的情郎。这里头没有什么可惊怪的：此地风气如此。可是唉，诸神为什么不叫你留须，还把它卷起来，同别的男人一样？那样你早在两个钟头之前就已脱身、安然无事了。」

===Original===

“A smooth chin pleases me better.”

===Chinese===

「光下巴更合我的意。」

===Original===

“So it does me,” I heard her murmur as she leaned her weight on the stone which hung in the doorway, and pushed it ajar; “your chin.” The ragged men outside﻿—there were women with them also﻿—did not wait to watch me very closely. A coarse jest or two flew (which I could have found good heart to have repaid with a sword-thrust) and they stepped off into the darkness, just turning from time to time to make sure we followed. On all sides others were pressing in the same direction﻿—black shadows against the night; the rain spat noisily on the camp fires as we passed them; and from behind us came up others. There were no sleepers in the camp now; all were pressing on to hear this preacher who stood on the pedestal of the war engine; and if we had tried to swerve from the straight course, we should have been marked at once.

===Chinese===

「也合我的意，」我听见她一面把身子的分量倚在门洞里悬着的那块石头上、将它推开一条缝，一面喃喃地说，「你的下巴。」外头那些破衣烂衫的汉子——他们中间也有女人——并不等我细看。一两句粗鄙的玩笑飞了过来（我本很有兴致拿一剑回敬），他们便迈步进了黑暗，只是不时回一回头，确认我们跟随着。四面八方都有人朝同一个方向涌去——夜色里一道道黑色的影子；我们经过篝火时，雨点噼啪作响地啐在火上；我们身后又跟上更多的人。此刻营中再没有睡觉的人了；人人都涌着去听这位站在攻城重械座架上的布道者；我们若想偏离这条直路，立刻就会被人认出来。

===Original===

So we held on through the darkness, and presently came within earshot.

===Chinese===

于是我们在黑暗里继续走，不多时便到了听得见的地方。

===Original===

Still it was little enough of the preacher’s words we could make out at first. “Who are your chiefs?” came the question at the end of a fervid harangue, and immediately all further rational talk was drowned in uproar. “We have no chiefs,” the people shouted, “we are done with chiefs; we are all equal here. Take away your silly magic. You may kill us with magic if you choose, but rule us you shall not. Nor shall the other priests rule. Nor Phorenice. Nor anybody. We are done with rulers.”

===Chinese===

起初，布道者的话我们仍旧辨得出的寥寥无几。一篇炽烈的长篇演说收尾处，落下这么一问：『你们的首领是谁？』话音未落，一切进一步的理性言谈立时淹没在喧哗里。「我们没有首领，」人们吼道，「首领我们受够了；这里人人平等。收起你那套愚蠢的法术罢。你高兴，尽可用法术杀死我们，可统治我们，你们休想。别的祭司也休想统治。福伦尼刻也休想。谁都休想。统治者我们受够了。」

===Original===

The press had brought us closer and closer to the man who stood on the war engine. We saw him to be old, with white hair that tumbled on his shoulders, and a long white beard, untrimmed and uncurled. Save for a wisp of rag about the loins, his body was unclothed, and glistened in the wet.

===Chinese===

人潮把我们挤得离那站在攻城重械上的人越来越近。我们看出他是个老人，白发披散在肩头，一部长须也是白的，不加修剪，也不卷烫。除了腰间缠的一绺破布，他周身赤裸着，在雨水里泛着亮光。

===Original===

But in his hand he held that which marked his caste. With it he pointed his sentences, and at times he whirled it about bathing his wet, naked body in a halo of light. It was a wand whose tip burned with an unconsuming fire, which glowed and twinkled and blazed like some star sent down by the Gods from their own place in the high heaven. It was the Symbol of our Lord the Sun, a credential no one could forge, and one on which no civilised man would cast a doubt.

===Chinese===

但他手里握着一样标明他等第的东西。他拿它给自己的言辞加点，时时又把它抡舞起来，让自己湿淋淋的裸身浴在一环光环里。那是一支短杖，杖头燃着一团不焚毁自身的火，明灭闪烁、灼灼煌煌，像诸神从高天上自家的居所里遣下来的一颗星。那便是太阳神主的圣徽，一份无人能伪造的凭证，一件文明人绝不会对之起疑的信物。

===Original===

Indeed, the ragged frantic crew did not question for one moment that he was a member of the Clan of Priests, the Clan which from time out of numbering had given rulers for the land, and even in their loudest clamours they freely acknowledged his powers. “You may kill us with your magic, if you choose,” they screamed at him. But stubbornly they refused to come back to their old allegiance. “We have suffered too many things these later years,” they cried. “We are done with rulers now for always.”

===Chinese===

的确，这群衣衫褴褛的狂乱之众，一刻也不曾怀疑他是祭司氏族的一员——那氏族自不可胜数的远古以来，便为这片国土供给统治者；即便在他们最响亮的鼓噪里，他们也坦然承认他的神通。「你高兴，尽可用你的法术杀死我们，」他们朝他尖声喊叫。可他们顽固地拒绝归回旧日的效忠。「这些年来我们吃的苦太多了，」他们喊道，「如今我们同统治者，是永远一刀两断了。」

===Original===

But for myself I saw the old man with a different emotion. Here was Zaemon that was father to Nais, Zaemon that had seen me yesterday seated on the divan at Phorenice’s elbow, and who today could denounce me as Deucalion if so he chose. These rebels had expended a navy in their wish to kill me four days earlier, and if they knew of my nearness, even though Nais were my advocate, her cold reasoning would have had little chance of an audience now. The High Gods who keep the tether of our lives hide Their secrets well, but I did not think it impious to be sure that mine was very near the cutting then.

===Chinese===

至于我自己，我看这老人，怀着的是另一种心绪。这正是泽蒙，娜伊丝的父亲；正是这泽蒙，昨天还看见我坐在福伦尼刻肘边的软榻上，今天只要他愿意，便能告发我是德乌卡利翁。四天以前，这些叛党怀着杀我的念想，不惜折损了一整支舰队；他们若知道我近在咫尺，哪怕娜伊丝替我辩护，她那冷静的条陈，此刻也难有被人听完的份。拴着我们性命的至上诸神，把自己的秘密藏得严实；但我彼时认定，我那条拴绳已临近割断——这念头，我并不以为渎神。

===Original===

The beautiful woman saw this too. She even went so far as to twine her fingers in mine and press them as a farewell, and I pressed hers in return, for I was sorry enough not to see her more. Still I could not help letting my thoughts travel with a grim gloating over the fine mound of dead I should build before these ragged, unskilled rebels pulled me down. And it was inevitable this should be so. For of all the emotions that can ferment in the human heart, the joy of strife is keenest, and none but an old fighter, face to face with what must necessarily be his final battle, can tell how deep this lust is embroidered into the very foundations of his being.

===Chinese===

这美人也看出了这一层。她甚至把手指同我的交缠在一处，作诀别似的一握，我也回握了她的手，因为再见不到她更多，我心里的怅惘也着实不轻。可我仍旧禁不住让心思带着一腔阴惨惨的得意，飞到那座漂亮的尸山上去——那是我要在这些褴褛无艺的叛党把我扑倒之前堆起来的。而事情必然如此。因为人心之中所能发酵的一切情绪里，争斗之欢是最锋利的一种；唯有惯战的老兵，面对面站在必是自己最后一战跟前，才说得清这股欲念绣进他生命根基里的针脚有多深。

===Original===

But for the time Zaemon did not see me, being too much wrapped in his outcry, and so I was free to listen to the burning words which he spread around him, and to determine their effect on the hearers.

===Chinese===

但泽蒙一时没有看见我，他太沉浸在自己的疾呼里；于是我得以从容细听他向四周播撒的那些火烫的字句，并察看它们在听者身上激起的效用。

===Original===

The theme he preached was no new one. He told that ever since the beginning of history, the Gods had set apart one Clan of the people to rule over the rest and be their Priests, and until the coming of Phorenice these had done their duties with exactitude and justice. They had fought invaders, carried war against the beasts, and studied earth-movements so that they were able to foretell earthquakes and eruptions, and could spread warnings that the people might be able to escape their devastations. They are no self-seekers; their aim was always to further the interest of Atlantis, and so do honour to the kingdom on which the High Gods had set their special favour. Under the Priestly Clan, Atlantis had reached the pinnacle of human prosperity and happiness.

===Chinese===

他布讲的题目不是什么新鲜玩意。他说，自有史以来，诸神便从万民中分别出一族，来统御其余的人，做他们的祭司；直到福伦尼刻到来之前，这一族都一丝不苟、公正无私地尽了他们的职分。他们抗击过来犯之敌，向野兽兴兵开战，研习大地的动静，因而能够预言地震与喷发，传布警讯，使百姓得以逃脱那些灾殃的蹂躏。他们不是营私自利之徒；他们的目标从来是增进亚特兰蒂斯的利益，为这个蒙至上诸神特别眷顾的王国增光。在祭司氏族的治下，亚特兰蒂斯登上了人间繁荣与安乐的极顶。

===Original===

“But,” cried the old man, waving the Symbol till his wet body glistened in a halo of light, “the people grew fat and careless with their easy life. They began to have a conceit that their good fortune was earned by their own puny brains and thews, and was no gift from the Gods above; and presently the cult of these Gods became neglected, and Their temples were barren of gifts and worshippers. Followed a punishment. The Gods in Their inscrutable way decreed that a wife of one of the Priests (that was a governor of no inconsiderable province) should see a woman child by the wayside, and take it for adoption. That child the Gods in their infinite wisdom fashioned into a scourge for Atlantis, and you who have felt the weight of Phorenice’s hand, know with what completeness the High Gods can fashion their instruments.

===Chinese===

「可是，」老人喊道，一面挥动圣徽，直到他湿淋淋的身子在一环光环里闪闪发亮，「百姓在安逸的日子里养肥了，也养得大意了。他们起了自负之心，以为自己的好运是自己渺小的脑髓和筋骨挣下的，并非上头诸神所赐；不久，对这些神的敬奉便遭了冷落，他们的庙宇里再无供品，也无礼拜者。随之而来的是一场惩罚。诸神以他们不可测度的方式降下旨意：叫一位祭司之妻（那祭司是个不小行省的总督）在路旁看见一个女婴，收来抚养。诸神以他们无穷的智慧，把那孩子打造成了亚特兰蒂斯的一条鞭子；你们这些领教过福伦尼刻手掌分量的人，都知道至上诸神打造起他们的器物来，手艺是何等完备。」

===Original===

“Yet, even as they set up, so can they throw down, and those that shall debase Phorenice are even now appointed. The old rule is to be reestablished; but not till you who have sinned are sufficiently chastened to cry to it for relief.” He waved the mysterious glowing Symbol before him. “See,” he cried in his high old quavering voice, “you know the unspeakable Power of which that is the sign, and for which I am the mouthpiece. It is for you to make decision now. Are the Gods to throw down this woman who has scorned Them and so cruelly trodden on you? Or are you to be still further purged of your pride before you are ripe for deliverance?”
